== Appended: the step convergence's cause (Task 5's trace) ==
Task 5, Step 3: the cause of the drive's step dependence (gap 0, Gugan's mallet, guideEffort 0, 3 m/s)

Traced with <temp>/scripts/task5-trace1..5.ts (focused throwaway scripts, foreground), at dt 2.5, 5 and 10 µs.

1. The figure. Ratio 5.1702 / 5.1819 / 5.2009 (spread 0.031 > 0.02). The croqueted ball's travel is step-free (5.9012 /
   5.9009 / 5.9014 m); the striker's ball's is not (1.1414 / 1.1388 / 1.1347 m). After the strike (3.1 ms) the striker
   flies with v_y 1.5531 / 1.5533 / 1.5537, v_z 0.10375 / 0.10250 / 0.09977 m/s and spin w_x -2.1461 / -2.1135 / -2.0367
   rad/s: the spin (and the hop) differ, first order or worse in dt.

2. Where. The strike's impulses on the striker over 0-3.2 ms: the normal impulses agree to 1e-4 (face 2.37567 / 2.37576
   / 2.37598, ball-ball 1.66990 / 1.66988 / 1.66992 N·s). The tangential ones do not: face J_T,z 3.4664e-2 / 3.4230e-2 /
   3.3252e-2, ball-ball -1.7555e-2 / -1.7404e-2 / -1.7095e-2 N·s. Angular impulse about the striker's centre, total
   -0.845e-3 / -0.832e-3 / -0.802e-3 N·m·s, i.e. Δw_x 0.034 and 0.079 rad/s with I = 0.4·m·R²: the whole spin
   difference.

3. The first quantity that differs beyond the step's scaling: the ball-ball pair's slide-to-stick transition. The pair
   slides from its entry step and re-sticks at 457.5 / 450 / 430 µs; less the contact's start-time offset (about dt,
   2.8 / 5.3 / 10.3 µs) that is 454.7 / 444.7 / 419.7 µs, 10 and 35 µs apart (2-3.5 steps). At the re-stick the slip is
   still large and scales with dt: slip_z ≈ 1.2e-2 m/s at 5 µs, ≈ 2.5e-2 m/s at 10 µs (trace5). After it the pair's force
   falls below the cone (at 460 µs: |T| 68.32 vs μN 70.42 N at 5 µs; 61.71 vs 68.89 N at 10 µs), and the deficit is
   carried through the rest of the contact into the striker's spin; the face's J_T follows through the coupled motion
   (the face sticks until 1.3 ms and slides only at its end; it has no slide-to-stick transition).

4. Mechanism (contactLaw.ts tangentialForce, Cundall-Strack). A sliding step resets the spring to carry the cone it
   met, k_t·|ξ| = μ·N_old. The next step's trial is -k_t·(ξ + slip·dt) - c_t·slip, against μ·N_new. With the slip still
   along the spring, |trial| = μ·N_old + (k_t·dt + c_t)·|slip|, so the pair sticks as soon as
       |slip| ≤ μ·(N_new - N_old) / (k_t·dt + c_t) ≈ μ·Ṅ·dt / c_t     (c_t ≫ k_t·dt: 106 vs 5-10 N·s/m),
   a slip threshold proportional to the step (μ·Ṅ ≈ 3.2e5 N/s here: 1.4e-2 m/s at 5 µs, 2.7e-2 at 10 µs, as observed).
   In the continuum the same law re-sticks only when the slip vanishes or reverses (|k_t·ξ + c_t·slip| > μN for any
   slip along ξ when k_t·|ξ| = μN). The discrete law re-sticks early because the cone grew within the step while the
   spring still holds last step's cone. That is the lead's "stick/slip branch", but at the slide-to-stick transition
   after the entry's slide, not on the entry step itself.

5. Fix (Step 4): a pair that slid on its previous step and would now stick is re-tested with its carried spring held
   at the current cone (k_t·|ξ| = μN); it keeps sliding unless that trial too lies within the cone, i.e. until its slip
   no longer runs along the spring. A step that slides today, and every pair that did not slide on its previous step,
   is computed exactly as today (the branch is taken only on slid && would-stick). The default step is unchanged.
   Scope: the impact's applyPair pairs (face-ball, ball-ball, ball-obstacle). The turf bed (turfBed.ts) and head-turf
   (headTurf.ts) call tangentialForce with the same reset and are left as they are: the trace shows their impulses
   step-free here (turf/blue J_N 3.10e-3 / 3.13e-3 / 3.20e-3 N·s, a few % of the strike's).
